● I've read the mockup and traced both existing report features. Key finding
  before I ask:

  The SAR backend already models the mockup's Screen 1 + 3. Cycle carries
  company_profile, sector (bank/insurance/general/reit/finance_co), is_shariah,
  has_subsidiaries, has_sukuk — the exact issuer-profile drivers — and
  sarCycles.resolveSections() already computes the section list with layer
  (common/cma/sector/optional), mode, and status.
  CycleSection.content/attachment exist but nothing in this repo renders them.
  There is no endpoint for source-document intake or an assembled annual report
  in this frontend (that lives in the external SAR app at :3000).

  Design-wise, "like the existing report" = the app's own system, not the
  mockup's: #4040C8 indigo, Plus Jakarta Sans + DM Mono for numbers,
  .card/.btn.bp/.badge classes from index.css, and the 5-step
  QuarterlyReportStepper.

● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Where should the Annual Board Report Builder live? → Extend existing 
     cycle pages (Recommended)
     · What data should it run on? → Mockup fixture data for now
     · How much of the mockup's report-styling controls do you want? → Reuse the
     app's existing cover/brand pickers (Recommended)
     · Do you want inline content editing and PDF/DOCX export on the report 
     preview? → Reuse quarterly's editing + export

● Plan

  One page, one route, maximum reuse. Everything visual comes from components
  that already ship.

  New files (2)

  src/pages/annual-report/boardReport.ts — the mockup's logic + data, ported as
  plain data:
  - IssuerProfile (type / sector / sharia / rated / capinst)
  - SECTION_REGISTRY — BR01–BR43 with req (M/O/C), src, grp,
  variant/finOnly/condition flags
  - resolveSection / reqOf / noteFor / countSections — the mockup's resolver,
  unchanged semantics
  - SOURCE_DOCS, COMPANY_FIXTURES (Wafrah Healthcare + Meridian Bank),
  directors/committees/remuneration/related-party
  - buildReportSections(profile): ProducedSection[] — emits quarterly-shaped
  sections: prose → mode:'generate', tables → mode:'table' with {rows}/{tables}
  JSON

  That last line is the whole trick: shaping fixtures as ProducedSection means
  SectionContent, EditableSectionContent, FinancialTable and GenericTable render
  the entire report with zero new renderers.

  src/pages/annual-report/BoardReportPage.tsx — 4 steps (Profile · Sources ·
  Sections · Report) as internal state, not 4 routes. Fixtures aren't persisted,
  so separate routes would need a store for no gain. Same shell as
  AssembledReportPage: stepper → toolbar → scrolling body → footer nav.

  Touched files (4)

  ┌────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │            File            │                   Change                    │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ QuarterlyReportStepper.tsx │ optional steps?: string[], reportId         │
  │                            │ optional — 3 lines                          │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                            │ one lazy route                              │
  │ App.tsx                    │ /annual-report/cycles/:cycleId/board-report │
  │                            │  inside the existing admin/ir block         │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ CycleDetailPage.tsx        │ one Board report → button in the header     │
  │                            │ actions                                     │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ index.css                  │ small @media print block so Save-as-PDF     │
  │                            │ prints the document only                    │
  └────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  AppLayout needs nothing — the /annual-report prefix already resolves the
  topbar title.

  Reused verbatim

  CoverRenderer, CoverTemplatePicker, SectionContent, EditableSectionContent,
  Spinner, quarterlyReports.getColorPalettesGlobal(), and the .card / .btn.bp /
  .badge b-* / .tabs / .fw-chip vocabulary. Theme is the app's: #4040C8, Plus
  Jakarta Sans, DM Mono for figures — none of the mockup's violet/Fraunces.

  Corners I'm cutting deliberately

  - Director profiles → table, not the mockup's CV cards. You declined the
  CV-layout switcher; add a card renderer later if the cards matter.
  - Export = Print / Save PDF (window.print() + print stylesheet). There is no
  annual export endpoint — I won't ship a DOCX button that 404s.
  - Section edits live in page state. Fixture data has nowhere to save to;
  swapping onSave for an API call later is a one-liner.
  - Cover templates = local classic/bold/minimal (the three CoverRenderer
  actually implements); palettes come from the real global endpoint with
  FALLBACK_COLOR_PALETTES as backup.

  Approve and I'll build it.

● Update(src\index.css)  ⎿  Added 9 lines
      1099  ═══════════════════════════════════════════ */
      1100  @media print {
      1101    html, body, #root { height: auto; overflow: visible; backgr
            ound: #fff; }
      1102 +  /* The shell is `position: fixed` and `.main` clips its ove
           +rflow — leave either                                         
      1103 +     in place and the absolutely-positioned document below is
           + clipped to one                                              
      1104 +     screen. Unwind both, and drop the chrome outright rather
           + than merely                                                 
      1105 +     hiding it, so it contributes no blank pages. */

● Built, linted, 11 tests passing.

  What shipped

  /annual-report/cycles/:cycleId/board-report — reached from a Board report →
  button on the cycle detail header. Four steps in one page (Profile · Sources ·
  Sections · Report), all in the app's own theme: #4040C8, Plus Jakarta Sans,
  DM Mono figures, .card/.btn/.badge classes. None of the mockup's
  violet/Fraunces.

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │                    File                     │                            │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │                                             │ new — BR01–BR43 registry,  │
  │ src/pages/annual-report/boardReport.ts      │ resolver, source docs,     │
  │                                             │ issuer fixtures,           │
  │                                             │ buildReportSections()      │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ src/pages/annual-report/BoardReportPage.tsx │ new — the four steps       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ src/test/board-report-resolver.test.ts      │ new — 11 tests on the      │
  │                                             │ resolver                   │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ QuarterlyReportStepper.tsx                  │ steps?: string[] override, │
  │                                             │  reportId optional         │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ App.tsx · CycleDetailPage.tsx · index.css   │ route · entry button ·     │
  │                                             │ print block                │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  The report body is emitted as ProducedSection[] — the same shape the quarterly
  endpoints return — so SectionContent, EditableSectionContent, CoverRenderer
  and CoverTemplatePicker render and edit it unchanged. Zero new renderers. Real
  data where it exists: cycle fiscal year for the cover period, company
  brand_colors, the global palette endpoint — all best-effort, none of them
  fatal.

  Two bugs I caught reviewing my own work: .app-shell is position: fixed, so the
  print rule would have clipped the document to one screen (now unwound, chrome
  display: none rather than merely invisible); and body sections were numbered
  01, 02… while the contents number all ~30 resolved sections in a different
  order — they now show their registry code instead of a sequence that would
  contradict the contents.

  Skipped: director profiles render as a table, not CV cards — add a card
  renderer if you want the mockup's look. Export is Print / Save PDF only; DOCX
  needs a backend job, and I won't ship a button that 404s. Section edits live
  in page state and reset when you change the profile (the toolbar says so) —
  swap handleSave for an API call when there's somewhere to save to.

● Lint and build clean.

  Reports → Board Report is now a direct sidebar child, at /board-report.
  Refresh and it'll be under Quarterly / Earnings / Annual.

  Two entry points, one page:
  - /board-report — sidebar, no cycle needed. Cover period defaults to the last
  completed financial year; Back exits to the cycles list.
  - /annual-report/cycles/:cycleId/board-report — the Board report → button on a
  cycle, which seeds the cover period from that cycle's fiscal year and exits
  back to it.

  I gave it its own top-level path rather than nesting under /annual-report
  because the Annual sidebar item matches on prefix — nested, opening the
  builder would have highlighted both items at once.

✻ Baked for 1m 40s

❯  Two entry points, one page:                                                  
    - /board-report — sidebar, no cycle needed. Cover period                    
    defaults to the last completed financial year; Back exits to the            
    cycles list.                                                                
    - /annual-report/cycles/:cycleId/board-report — the Board report

    cycle's fiscal year and exits back to it.                                   
   i dont understand                                                            

● There are two doors into the same screen. That's it.

  Door 1 — the sidebar (what you asked for). Reports → Board Report. Opens the
  builder straight away.

  Door 2 — from a cycle. Reports → Annual → click a cycle → a Board report →
  button on that page. Same screen, just opened from inside a specific reporting
  cycle.

  The only difference between them: the cover page needs a year on it. Coming
  from a cycle, it uses that cycle's fiscal year. Coming from the sidebar
  there's no cycle to ask, so it writes last year. Nothing else differs — same
  four steps, same report.

  I built door 2 first (that was the original plan), then added door 1 when you
  said it must be under Reports. If you only want one, say which and I'll delete
  the other — the sidebar one alone is probably cleaner.

● The purple Ask Centriyon button is glued to the bottom-right corner of every
  page and floats on top of everything.

  - Before: the page stopped ~130px short of the bottom, so nothing could ever
  end up hidden under it. That was the empty gap you didn't like.
  - Now: the page runs all the way down — which means the footer bar passes
  underneath that floating button.
  - So: I moved Sources → 176px in from the right edge. It now sits next to the
  floating button instead of under it, where you couldn't click it.

  That's the only reason it isn't flush with the right edge. If you'd rather
  have it flush and accept the overlap, say so and I'll drop the offset.

❯ where the actual options come from                                            

● Almost all of it is hardcoded by me in src/pages/annual-report/boardReport.ts,
  ported from the mockup HTML. That was the call you made earlier — "Mockup
  fixture data for now."

  Hardcoded (fixture):

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │              On screen               │              Source              │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Issuer type, Sector, the 3 yes/no    │ ISSUER_TYPES / ISSUER_SECTORS —  │
  │ flags                                │ boardReport.ts:44-56             │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Financial year dropdown              │ computed, current year and 7     │
  │                                      │ back                             │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ All 43 sections + M/O/C + data owner │ SECTION_REGISTRY —               │
  │                                      │ boardReport.ts:105-149           │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ What drops / re-frames / is N/A      │ resolveSection / reqOf / noteFor │
  │                                      │  — boardReport.ts:196-227        │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ The 11 source documents and what     │ SOURCE_DOCS —                    │
  │ they feed                            │ boardReport.ts:260-272           │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Every word of the report — Wafrah    │ COMPANY_FIXTURES, DIRECTORS,     │
  │ Healthcare, Meridian Bank, the       │ REMUNERATION… —                  │
  │ directors, remuneration, attendance, │ boardReport.ts:290-560           │
  │  five-year table                     │                                  │
  └──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  Actually from the backend (3 things, all on the cover):
  - companies.getMyCompany() → your saved brand_colors
  - quarterlyReports.getColorPalettesGlobal() → the palette presets in the cover
  picker
  - user.company_name from the auth context → the company name on the cover

  So the profile pills change the report because my resolver says so, not
  because a server resolved anything.

  Worth knowing for when you wire it up: SAR already has the real version of the
  first half. A Cycle carries company_profile, sector, is_shariah, has_sukuk,
  and sarCycles.resolveSections(cycleId) returns the real section list with
  layer/mode/status. But its sectors are bank | insurance | general | reit | 
  finance_co — not the mockup's industrial / retail / healthcare / real_estate /
  telecom / energy. Those two lists have to be reconciled before the profile
  step can talk to SAR. There's still no endpoint at all for document intake or
  the report body.

● The profile now seeds from GET /companies/me — the same values onboarding
  collected and annual-report cycles already inherit.

  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Field       │                    Company source                    │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                  │ reporting_sector === 'bank'; falls back to           │
  │ Issuer type      │ sector_name matching /bank/ only when                │
  │                  │ reporting_sector is unset                            │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                  │ keyword match on sector_name — "Health Care          │
  │ Sector           │ Equipment" → Healthcare, "Capital Goods" →           │
  │                  │ Industrial, "Oil & Gas" → Energy, etc.               │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Sharia-compliant │ is_shariah                                           │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Capital          │ has_sukuk                                            │
  │ instruments      │                                                      │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                  │ not prefilled — onboarding doesn't capture credit    │
  │ Externally rated │ ratings, so guessing it would silently add or drop   │
  │                  │ the Credit ratings section. Stays yours to set.      │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Anything missing or unmatched falls back to the previous default rather than
  to a wrong value.

  Two things worth knowing:
  - A green line at the top of the card says it was prefilled, so the answers
  don't look like they came from nowhere.
  - If you change an answer before /companies/me returns, your answer wins — a
  touchedRef guard stops a slow response overwriting it.

  profileFromCompany lives in boardReport.ts:60-96 and has 4 tests covering the
  mapping, the blank-company fallback, the bank precedence rule, and that rated
  is never guessed. 15 tests passing, build clean.

● Root cause: Healthcare wasn't read from Shell's record — it was my
  DEFAULT_PROFILE.sector fallback being presented as a prefill. The Sharia = Yes
  you can see was genuinely prefilled, which is why it looked like the whole
  thing worked.

  Three fixes:

  1. The sector is never guessed. IssuerProfile.sector is now IssuerSector | 
  null and DEFAULT_PROFILE.sector is null. An unmatched sector stays empty
  rather than silently becoming Healthcare — it names the fines regulator in
  BR21, so a wrong value is worse than no value. Continue is disabled on step 1
  until a corporate issuer picks one (banks aren't blocked; their sections don't
  vary by sector).

  2. The sector is actually looked up. sector_name is only present "when the
  backend joined it", so when it's absent the page now resolves sector_id
  against getSectors() and matches on that name.

  3. The keyword map was too narrow for a company like Shell. Energy now matches
  oil|gas|petro|refin|fuel|utilit|power|electric|renewab|mining, and it's
  ordered above industrial so an oil-and-chemicals company reads as energy, not
  industrial. Also widened healthcare, real estate, telecom, retail and
  industrial.

  The banner tells the truth now: green when the profile mapped cleanly, amber
  when it says your sector doesn't map to one of the six and asks you to pick
  the closest.

  If Shell still lands with no sector selected, that means the company record
  has no sector_id/sector_name at all — worth checking on the Profile page.

  17 tests pass (4 new ones on the null-sector behaviour); build clean. The only
  typecheck error is the pre-existing CycleForm.tsx:205.

✻ Brewed for 4m 17s

❯ how do ypu prefiil option ?                                                   

● One API call on page load, mapped onto the five profile fields.

  The chain (BoardReportPage.tsx:165-195):

  GET /api/v1/companies/me
     → company record
     → if sector_name is missing, GET /api/v1/lookups/sectors and find sector_id
     → profileFromCompany(company, sectorName)
     → setProfile(...) + setPrefilled(true)

  The mapping (boardReport.ts:profileFromCompany):

  ┌───────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │       Pill        │                        Rule                         │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                   │ reporting_sector === 'bank' → Bank. If              │
  │ Issuer type       │ reporting_sector is unset, falls back to /\bbank/i  │
  │                   │ on the sector name.                                 │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Sector            │ regex list against the sector name — first match    │
  │                   │ wins, null if none match                            │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Sharia-compliant  │ is_shariah straight through                         │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Capital           │ has_sukuk straight through                          │
  │ instruments       │                                                     │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Externally rated  │ not touched — no company field exists               │
  ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Financial year    │ not touched — no company field; defaults to last    │
  │                   │ year                                                │
  └───────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The sector regexes (boardReport.ts:59-66), first match wins so order matters:

  /health|pharma|medical|hospital|biotech|life scien/  → healthcare
  /real.?estate|propert|reit|construction|contracting/ → real_estate
  /telecom|communicat|media|internet|software|technolog/ → telecom
  /energy|oil|gas|petro|refin|fuel|utilit|power|electric|renewab|mining/ →
  energy
  /retail|consumer|food|beverage|staples|hospitality|hotel/ → retail
  /industr|material|manufactur|capital
  goods|transport|logistic|chemical|cement|steel/ → industrial

  Two guards:
  - Anything the company record doesn't answer keeps its default — never a
  guess. That's why rated and the unmatched sector stay empty.
  - A touchedRef freezes the seed the moment you click any pill, so a slow
  /companies/me can't overwrite an answer you already gave.

  The values themselves come from onboarding — the same company_profile /
  is_shariah / has_sukuk / reporting_sector fields an annual-report cycle
  inherits, editable on the Profile page.

● The Sector pills now come from GET /api/v1/lookups/sectors — the same table
  onboarding and the Profile page use. My six invented sectors are deleted.

  What changed:
  - IssuerProfile.sector is now a string | null holding the sector's name as the
  lookup table spells it, not a fixed enum.
  - Prefill stores the company's sector verbatim — sector_name, or the row its
  sector_id points at. No re-bucketing, so what you see is what's on the company
  record.
  - The page loads /companies/me and /lookups/sectors together, since the second
  is both the picklist and how the first's sector_id gets a name.
  - If the lookup fails you get the error, and if it's empty you get "No sectors
  are configured" — never a fallback list.

  The one place the old six survive is SECTOR_REGULATOR, and only to answer "who
  levies the fines in BR21". regulatorForSector(name) keyword-matches the
  lookup name onto a regulator — SFDA, CITC, REGA, MoIMR — and returns the
  generic "the sector regulator" when it doesn't recognise it. They're no longer
  a picklist, and the type is renamed RegulatorGroup to stop that being
  confused again.

  18 tests pass, including one asserting an unmapped sector never invents a
  regulator. Build clean.

❯ issuer ype and Discloure flags where they come from?                          

● Different answers for the two.

  Issuer type

  Options: invented by me. ISSUER_TYPES in boardReport.ts:44-47 — just two
  labels I wrote, "Bank (SAMA-regulated)" and "Non-financial (corporate)".

  Value: real. Prefilled from company.reporting_sector === 'bank'.

  There's a mismatch worth knowing about. reporting_sector isn't a boolean in
  the DB — it's SAR's five codes:

  bank | insurance | general | reit | finance_co
  (types/cycles.ts:177-183)

  My toggle flattens all five into bank / not-bank. That's defensible for the
  registry — the bank-only sections are SAMA returns, Basel RWA, customer
  deposits, IFRS 9 ECL, which genuinely only apply to a bank — but it means an
  insurance company or a finance company shows as "Non-financial (corporate)",
  which is wrong on its face even if the resolved sections happen to be right.

  Disclosure flags

  Not a picklist — three booleans, and they split three ways:

  ┌───────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │             Flag              │                 Source                  │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Sharia-compliant              │ Real — company.is_shariah               │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Regulatory capital            │ Real — company.has_sukuk                │
  │ instruments (Tier 1 sukuk)    │                                         │
  ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Externally rated              │ Nothing. No such field anywhere. Pure   │
  │                               │ UI state, defaults to No.               │
  └───────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So two of the three round-trip to the company record; the third is a question
  only this page asks.

  One more thing: the company also carries has_subsidiaries, which I'm not
  reading. BR19 "Subsidiaries & associates" is currently always included — it
  could be gated on that field the way BR34 is gated on Sharia.

  Two things I can fix if you want:
  1. Issuer type from the real five codes — show the company's actual
  reporting_sector and treat only bank as triggering the bank-only sections, so
  an insurer reads as "Insurance" instead of "Non-financial".
  2. Wire has_subsidiaries to BR19.

  Say which.

✻ Cogitated for 23s
❯ Two things I can fix if you want:                                             
    1. Issuer type from the real five codes — show the company's                
    actual reporting_sector and treat only bank as triggering the               
    bank-only sections, so an insurer reads as "Insurance" instead              
    of "Non-financial".                                                         
    2. Wire has_subsidiaries to BR19.                                           
   explain                                                                      

● 1. Issuer type from the real five codes

  Today the pills are two: Bank (SAMA-regulated) / Non-financial (corporate).
  The company record's reporting_sector actually holds one of five values.

  ┌────────────────────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────┐
  │ Company's reporting_sector │   Shows today   │   Would show    │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ bank                       │ Bank ✓          │ Bank            │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ insurance                  │ Non-financial ✗ │ Insurance       │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ general                    │ Non-financial ✓ │ General         │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ reit                       │ Non-financial ✗ │ REIT            │
  ├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ finance_co                 │ Non-financial ✗ │ Finance Company │
  └────────────────────────────┴─────────────────┴─────────────────┘

  Four of the five display wrong. The sections are still right — the bank-only
  ones (SAMA balances, Basel RWA, customer deposits, IFRS 9 ECL) genuinely apply
  only to a bank, and only bank would switch them on either way. So this is a
  labelling fix: an insurer stops being told it's non-financial. Five pills
  instead of two, bank remains the only one that changes the registry.

  2. Wire has_subsidiaries to BR19

  BR19 is "Subsidiaries & associates". It's currently marked mandatory and
  always appears — so a company with no subsidiaries gets a mandatory section
  about subsidiaries it doesn't have, and the fixture fills it in anyway.

  The company record already answers this: has_subsidiaries, set at onboarding.
  Wiring it makes BR19 conditional, exactly like BR34 (Sharia committee) and
  BR27 (Credit ratings) already are:

  - Has subsidiaries → BR19 included, as now.
  - Doesn't → BR19 shows on the Sections screen as N/A, greyed with the reason
  "only if the issuer has subsidiaries or associates", and drops out of the
  report and contents.

  Cost is one line in the registry plus reading the field in the prefill. The
  judgment call is that BR19 is currently req: 'M' and this makes it 'C' — if
  CMA requires the section to appear stating "none", that's the wrong move and
  I'd leave it alone.

  Want both?

※ recap: Goal: build the Annual Board Report builder at /board-report, styled like the Earnings setup page. It's working and building clean. I asked whether to make Issuer type show the company's real five sector codes and to gate BR19 on has_subsidiaries; waiting on your yes or no.

❯ issuer type is correct there will be two option i want to know that when      
  prefilling how you know that which option                                     

● boardReport.ts:106-111, one line decides it:

  const name = sectorName ?? c.sector_name ?? '';
  const isBank = c.reporting_sector === 'bank' || (!c.reporting_sector &&
  /\bbank/i.test(name));

  Read in order:

  1. reporting_sector === 'bank' → Bank. This is the authority. It's the
  company's own field, set at onboarding, one of bank | insurance | general | 
  reit | finance_co. Any of the other four → Non-financial.
  2. Fallback, only if reporting_sector is null or empty — look at the sector
  name from the lookup and test it for the word "bank". A company whose sector
  reads "Banks" or "Commercial Banking" → Bank. This exists because
  reporting_sector isn't always populated on older records.
  3. Neither → Non-financial, the default.

  Note step 2 only runs when step 1 has nothing to say. An explicit
  reporting_sector: 'general' beats a sector name of "Banks" — the field wins
  over the keyword, never the other way round. There's a test pinning that
  (board-report-resolver.test.ts, "reads bank off the sector name only when
  reporting_sector is unset").

  For Shell: reporting_sector is whatever onboarding set — if it's anything but
  bank, or it's empty and the sector name has no "bank" in it, you get
  Non-financial. Which is right.

  The one consequence to be aware of: finance_co is SAMA-regulated but resolves
  to Non-financial, so it won't get the bank-only sections (SAMA balances, Basel
  RWA, customer deposits, IFRS 9 ECL). You've said two options is correct, so
  I'm leaving that as-is — flagging it only so it isn't a surprise later.

✻ Cooked for 19s

❯ it means if reporting sector i sbank you select bank option and if it is not  
  bank you selcect coperate offer?                                              

● Yes. That's exactly it.

  - reporting_sector === 'bank' → Bank pill selected
  - anything else (insurance, general, reit, finance_co) → Non-financial 
  (corporate) pill selected

  The only extra case: if reporting_sector is empty on the record, it falls back
  to checking whether the sector name contains "bank" — and if that finds
  nothing, it lands on Non-financial too.

✻ Cooked for 7s

❯ so it is coorect all other reporting sector comes under corporate?            

● Correct given what the registry models — but it's worth being precise about
  what that hides.

  The registry only knows two shapes: bank and not-bank. The mockup only
  modelled those, so every bank-only section is SAMA/Basel/core-banking specific
  — SAMA balances, capital adequacy, customer deposits, loans & NPLs, IFRS 9
  ECL, MSME lending. For general and most corporates, dropping all of that is
  right.

  Two places it's thin:

  finance_co — finance companies are SAMA-licensed and do have capital-adequacy
  requirements and IFRS 9 expected-credit-loss on their loan book. They don't
  take deposits, so BR07c is correctly dropped, but BR08 (capital adequacy) and
  BR14 (ECL) arguably shouldn't be. Today they get neither.

  insurance and reit — these don't need bank sections, so dropping them is
  right, but they need sections the registry doesn't contain at all: technical
  provisions and solvency margin for an insurer, unit/fund disclosures for a
  REIT. Treating them as corporate isn't wrong so much as incomplete.

  So: the mapping is doing the right thing with the registry it has. The gap is
  in the registry, which is fixture data I ported from the mockup — it was never
  built to cover insurers, REITs or finance companies.

  I'd also flag that I'm reasoning from the section names, not from CMA/SAMA
  disclosure rules — worth having whoever owns the reporting requirements
  confirm the finance-company case before it matters.

  If you want, I can add the two conditional rules for finance_co — but that
  needs the issuer type to carry all five codes, which you said you didn't want.
  Your call.

❯ # Board of Directors' Report — API                                            
                                                                                
  Base URL: `/api/v1/board`                                                     
  Auth: `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>` on every endpoint. All access is scoped   
  to the caller's                                                               
  company — a report belonging to another company returns **404**, never 403.   
                                                                                
  The report itself is a `reports` row with `report_type='board_pack'` and      
  `period='FY-YYYY'`.                                                           
                                                                                
  The four screens of the builder map to the endpoints like this:               
                                                                                
  | Screen | Endpoints |                                                        
  |---|---|                                                                     
  | 1 · Issuer profile | `GET/PATCH /reports/{id}/profile` |                    
  | 2 · Source documents | `GET /reports/{id}/sources`, `POST                   
  .../sources/upload`, `DELETE .../sources/{document_id}` |                     
  | 3 · Resolved sections | `GET/PUT /reports/{id}/outline` |                   
  | 4 · Report | `POST .../produce`, `GET .../sections`, `GET .../assemble`,    
  `POST .../export` |                                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ## 1. Create and list                                                         
                                                                                
  ### `POST /board/reports` → 201                                               
                                                                                
  ```json                                                                       
  { "company_id": "uuid", "fiscal_year": 2025, "issuer_profile": null }         
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  `issuer_profile` is optional — omit it and the profile is seeded from the     
  company record                                                                
  (`reporting_sector` → issuer type, `is_shariah`, `has_sukuk`, sector).        
                                                                                
  ```json                                                                       
  {                                                                             
    "report_id": "uuid",                                                        
    "period": "FY-2025",                                                        
    "status": "draft",                                                          
    "issuer_profile": {                                                         
      "issuer_type": "corporate",                                               
      "sector": "healthcare",                                                   
      "sharia_compliant": false,                                                
      "externally_rated": false,                                                
      "has_capital_instruments": false                                          
    }                                                                           
  }                                                                             
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  **409** if an unfinished board report already exists for that company and     
  year — the body                                                               
  carries `existing_report_id` so the UI can offer "continue" instead of        
  "create".                                                                     
                                                                                
  ### `GET /board/reports?company_id=uuid`                                      
                                                                                
  ```json                                                                       
  { "reports": [                                                                
    { "report_id": "uuid", "period": "FY-2025", "status": "draft",              
      "created_at": "...", "updated_at": "...", "issuer_profile": { } }         
  ] }                                                                           
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ## 2. Screen 1 — issuer profile                                               
                                                                                
  ### `GET /board/reports/{report_id}/profile`                                  
  ### `PATCH /board/reports/{report_id}/profile`                                
                                                                                
  PATCH body is the profile itself. All five keys are accepted; `issuer_type`   
  is required and                                                               
  must be `bank` | `insurer` | `corporate`. The three flags must be real        
  booleans.                                                                     
                                                                                
  ```json                                                                       
  { "issuer_type": "bank", "sector": "healthcare",                              
    "sharia_compliant": true, "externally_rated": false,                        
    "has_capital_instruments": true }                                           
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Both return:                                                                  
                                                                                
  ```json                                                                       
  {                                                                             
    "report_id": "uuid",                                                        
    "issuer_profile": { },                                                      
    "derived": { "is_financial": true, "regulator": "sama" },                   
    "counts": { "included": 46, "mandatory": 35, "optional": 7,                 
                "conditional": 4, "dropped": 0, "na": 0 }                       
  }                                                                             
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  `derived` is computed, never stored. **PATCH re-resolves and re-saves the     
  ──── (373 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  ns that don't apply — don't hide them                                         
                                                                                
    The outline always returns all 46 sections, even ones that don't belong in  
    this report. Each one has a resolution telling you which:                   
                                                                                
    ┌────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    
    │ resolution │                      What to show                       │    
    ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    
    │ in         │ normal                                                  │    
    ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    
    │ variant    │ normal, plus the note explaining what's different       │    
    ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    
    │ dropped    │ greyed out, struck through — "this is a bank-only       │    
    │            │ section"                                                │    
    ├────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤    
    │ na         │ greyed out — "doesn't apply to you"                     │    
    └────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    
                                                                                
    It's tempting to filter the greyed ones out of the list. Don't. A           
    compliance officer looking at this screen needs to see "we left out         
    Capital adequacy because you're not a bank" — that's the reassurance the    
    screen exists to give. The note field carries that sentence, already        
    written.                                                                    
                                                                                
    Your mockup already did this correctly, with the "Hide non-applicable"      
    toggle.                                                                     
                                                                                
    ---                                                                         
    4. Two endpoints don't finish when they answer you                          
                                                                                
    Most endpoints do the work and reply with the result. Two don't, because    
    they take too long:                                                         
                                                                                
    - uploading documents — has to read and extract a PDF                       
    - produce all sections — has to write ~30 sections with AI                  
                                                                                
    These reply immediately with 202, which means "started, not done". The      
    reply contains a poll_url:                                                  
                                                                                
    { "run_id": "abc", "status": "running", "poll_url":                         
    "/api/v1/agent_runs/abc" }                                                  
                                                                                
    The frontend then checks that URL every few seconds until it says           
    completed or failed:                                                        
                                                                                
    const { poll_url } = await post(`/board/reports/${id}/produce`);            
    const timer = setInterval(async () => {                                     
      const run = await get(poll_url);                                          
      setProgress(run.output_summary);          // { produced: 12, skipped: 1,  
    total: 37 }                                                                 
      if (run.status !== "running") clearInterval(timer);                       
    }, 3000);                                                                   
                                                                                
    output_summary updates after each section finishes, so you can show a real  
    progress bar — "12 of 37 done" — instead of a spinner that sits there for   
    two minutes. thes are apis

● Two findings that shape the work:

  - SectionContent ignores columns. It derives table columns from row keys, so
  the board API's governance grids would render in the wrong order — and BR35's
  per-meeting columns (bare years/dates) hit a JS key-ordering trap that puts
  them before the Name column. That renderer needs extending, plus the matching
  columnsOf in EditableSectionContent.
  - computeProgress returns a flat 6% when a run emits no /nodes rows. For "12
  of 37 sections" I'd read output_summary directly rather than reuse it.

  Four things I can't resolve from the spec:

     /plan to edit
     Board Report: fixtures → the real /api/v1/board API

     Context

     The Annual Board Report builder currently runs entirely on fixture data I
     wrote from the
     mockup: a hardcoded 43-section registry, a client-side resolver, and a
     fabricated report body
     (src/pages/annual-report/boardReport.ts, ~720 lines). That was a deliberate
     stopgap — there
     was no backend.

     There is now. /api/v1/board owns the section registry (46 sections), the
     profile→section
     resolution, document intake, AI production, completion gating, approval and
     PDF/DOCX export.
     Everything the fixtures stood in for is real, plus four capabilities the
     builder never had:
     carried-forward confirmation, a completion gate, approve & lock, and real
     export.

     Outcome: delete the fixtures, move the resolver to the server, and wire all
     four screens plus
     the new lifecycle. The builder stops being a demo.

     Decisions already taken (do not revisit):
     1. Sector — pills stay wired to /api/v1/lookups/sectors; the chosen lookup
     name goes
     to PATCH /profile verbatim. No slug mapping, no hardcoded list.
     2. Issuer type — two pills, sending bank or corporate. insurer is never
     sent.
     This has a backend consequence; see Questions for the backend.
     3. Full replacement — every endpoint wired, including approve and export.
     4. Entry — /board-report becomes a setup page (year picker +
     existing-report tiles);
     the builder moves to /board-report/:reportId.

     ---
     Prerequisite: a live renderer defect

     src/components/quarterly/SectionContent.tsx ignores columns. NormTable has
     no such field
     and GenericTable derives columns from an Object.keys union of the rows.
     Consequences for
     the board API's governance grids:

     - the server's column order is discarded;
     - integer-like keys sort first, so a row
     {Metric:"Revenue","2025":…,"2024":…} renders as
     2024 | 2025 | Metric. BR35 (one column per board meeting) and BR18 (year
     columns) both hit
     this;
     - a column listed in columns but absent from every row silently vanishes;
     - TableBlock routes to FinancialTable on rows.some(r => 
     cell(r,'current_display','current','value')),
     so a grid with a column named value is misrendered as a two-column
     financial statement.

     This already affects the fixture BR18 today, so it ships independently of
     the API work.

     The fix (~10 lines, columns is a total discriminator — financial tables 
     never send it):

     - SectionContent.tsx: export asStringArray(v); add columns?: string[] to
     NormTable;
     populate it in the three record branches of normalizeTables
     (array-of-tables, parsed.tables,
     parsed.rows); change the shape test to const financial = !table.columns && 
     rows.some(...);
     pass columns into GenericTable, whose cols becomes columns ?? <existing 
     union>.
     - EditableSectionContent.tsx: mirror it — editableTables carries columns,
     and
     columnsOf(rows, explicit?) seeds its Set from explicit when given (still
     deleting
     HIDDEN_COLS). TableEditor deep-clones and re-serialises the whole parsed
     object, so
     columns round-trips through an edit untouched.

     Back-compatible: every current quarterly/earnings payload omits columns, so
     both expressions
     collapse to today's behaviour exactly.

     Leave the fallback Object.keys ordering alone with a ponytail: comment
     naming the trap —
     no payload without columns has numeric keys, and a stable-order rewrite is
     a bigger diff than
     the bug.

     Deleted

     The builder stays one page with four internal steps. Steps 1 and 3 have a
     hard data
     dependency (a profile PATCH re-resolves the outline server-side), and the
     shell/Block/Pill
     chrome is already written. A route-per-step split buys four duplicate
     bootstrap effects and a
     redirect table for a screen used once a year. If the file passes ~900 lines
     after wiring,
     extract the presentational primitives to board-ui.tsx — cycle-ui.tsx in the
     same folder is
     that convention already.

     ---
     API namespace

     request<T>() conventions: encodeURIComponent every id, multipart via
     {method:'POST', form: fd}
     (never set Content-Type; postForm is legacy), 202 is ok so the handle body
     returns directly.

     createReport / listReports / getProfile / patchProfile
     getSources / uploadSources(id, slot, files) / deleteSourceDocument
     getOutline / saveOutline
     produceSection(id, code, regenerate?) / produceAll / getSections /
     patchSectionContent
     getCompletion / confirmSection / approve / getAssemble / downloadExport

     downloadExport copies earnings.downloadEarningsExport (api.ts ~2536) —
     fetchWithAuth,
     !res.ok → parse detail/detail.error into a plain Error, then the
     /filename\*?=(?:UTF-8''|")?([^";]+)/i Content-Disposition read before a
     sanitised fallback.
     One difference: format is a query param, not a JSON body.

     Do not route the upload through postPipeline — it normalises 409 into a
     PipelineHandle
     and would swallow the existing_run_id the UI wants to act on. Plain request
     + form keeps
     ApiError.body reachable.

     ---
     Data flow

     Bootstrap

     One Promise.all([getProfile, getOutline, getSources, getCompletion]) on
     mount, cancelled
     flag, then setStep(initialStep(...)) behind a bootstrappedRef — approved →
     4, anything
     produced → 4, all required slots received → 3, else 1. A refresh must not
     dump you back on
     Profile. /assemble is deferred to step 4; it's heavy and invalid before
     production.

     Step 1 — profile

     PATCH on Continue, not per pill. PATCH /profile re-resolves and re-saves
     the whole
     outline, so a per-click PATCH would silently discard the user's
     include/order choices from
     step 3 every time they touched a flag. On Continue, if the draft differs
     from the saved echo:
     PATCH, then refetch outline and sources together — slot counts derive from
     issuer type
     (corporate 10, bank 11). Warn first if the outline has been customised.

     derived.regulator and counts from the response replace regulatorFor() and
     countSections() everywhere, including the actions-row summary. Delete the
     amber "your sector
     doesn't map to one of the six" notice — it's a lie once the server owns
     resolution.

     Step 2 — sources

     Render slots in the existing .uhead frame. required → M badge,
     feeds[].title for the
     "Feeds →" line, documents[] with extraction_status underneath. Replace =
     Remove then
     Upload; there's no replace endpoint, so make it two clicks rather than fake
     one.

     Upload is 202 + usePipelinePoll(run_id, poll_url). The hook has no
     completion callback, so a
     ref-guarded effect on phase/elapsedMs refetches sources every tick — that's
     what makes
     extraction_status move live (EarningsPreviewPage.tsx:161-182 is the
     precedent). Disable all
     other Upload buttons while a run is in flight (one pipeline per report); if
     a 409 lands anyway
     from another tab, adopt existing_run_id rather than showing an error the
     user can't act on.

     Step 3 — outline

     All 46 sections render, dropped/na greyed and struck through with their
     note — that's the
     reassurance the screen exists to give, and the reason the API returns them.
     Keep the existing
     "Hide non-applicable" toggle.

     Per row: excluded → no checkbox, no drag handle; M → checkbox checked and
     disabled
     (the server force-includes silently, so a live tick would visibly revert on
     next fetch);
     O/C → live checkbox. Drag-reorder among non-excluded rows.

     Autosave copies OutlinePage.tsx:427-472 — 700ms debounce, saveSeqRef
     latest-wins — minus the
     lock retry (board has no outline lock). On error, refetch: a 422 means
     nothing was saved, so
     server state is truth. outlinePayload sends all 46 in display order with
     their current
     included, including excluded rows at false; that's a no-op server-side and
     avoids betting on
     undocumented semantics for omitted sections.

     Step 4 — produce and report

     produceAll → 202 → poll. Do not use computeProgress — it flat-lines at 6%
     when a run
     emits no /nodes rows, which is this pipeline. Read run.output_summary
     through
     boardProduceSummary() (one cast, mirroring QuarterlyStatTiles in
     ProcessingPage:51-67) for
     "21 of 37 sections", with skipped and failed called out. Section rows use
     GenerateProgress's layout driven by the outline's status, refetched each
     tick.

     /assemble renders the document: CoverRenderer from cover.values +
     assemble.brand,
     contents from sections[] display_order (contiguous now, so the "codes not
     numbers" caveat
     goes), body through toBoardProduced → the existing EditableSectionContent.

     ▎ isCoverSection will not work here. It tests /cover/i against 
     ▎ section_code, and the
     ▎ board cover is BR01. /assemble returns the cover as a top-level key so 
     ▎ sections[] is
     ▎ probably already clean — but filter defensively on a parsed template_key,
     ▎ not on
     ▎ isCoverSection, or the cover's {template_key, values} renders as a 
     ▎ key/value table.

     Edits go to patchSectionContent, with real saving/error passed into
     EditableSectionContent instead of today's hardcoded false/null.
     Carried-forward sections
     (provenance === 'carried_forward' && !confirmed) get an amber "Still
     accurate?" strip →
     confirmSection. needs_input sections get a Produce button; a 422 renders
     "no producer for
     this section yet" inline — BR13/BR15/BR21 stay visible with an honest empty
     state rather
     than disappearing.

     ---
     Approve gate

     The 409 body is the completion payload, so one renderer serves both
     sources.

     A readiness strip sits above the document — {ready} of {total} ready plus
     an amber chip per
     non-empty blocker list, each scrolling to the first offending section (the
     arrays are section
     codes and every section already has a DOM anchor).

     Approve & Lock is enabled regardless of can_approve. Gating on a
     possibly-stale flag
     gives a dead button with no explanation; letting the 409 answer is one code
     path and always
     current. On 409, parse the body as a BoardCompletion, refresh the strip,
     and render the three
     blocker lists inside the open dialog via the new children slot, with titles
     resolved from
     the outline. On success, hide every pencil/Produce/Confirm control and show
     the Approved &
     Locked badge (AssembledReportPage.tsx:60-81 has the date formatter).

     Export via DownloadMenu with the new onDownload. Keep the window.print() 
     button — the
     @media print block keys on .print-doc/.print-hide, both preserved, and it's
     the fallback
     when export 409s on a partially-produced report.

     ---
     Tests

     Delete board-report-resolver.test.ts — 16 of its 18 cases test functions
     that no longer exist.
     The server owns the resolver; what's left worth testing is the client's own
     transformations and
     the two places it can silently corrupt server data.

     1. src/test/section-content-columns.test.tsx (highest value — a live 
     defect, not new code)
     — explicit order honoured; a column present in no row renders empty rather
     than vanishing;
     a grid with a value column isn't misrouted to FinancialTable; and the 
     regression guard:
     {rows} with current_display and no columns still renders financial.
     2. board-helpers.test.ts — profileFromCompany (bank→bank, all
     else→corporate, never
     insurer, sector verbatim including an unmapped name, externally_rated never
     guessed);
     initialStep per server state; outlinePayload order and completeness.
     3. __tests__/board-setup.test.tsx — a 409 on create surfaces "Continue
     existing report"
     pointing at existing_report_id. Mirrors earnings-setup.test.tsx.
     4. __tests__/board-report-approve.test.tsx — approve rejecting
     409-with-completion renders
     the blocker lists and does not mark the report approved.
     5. EditableSectionContent round-trip — editing one cell of a grid with
     columns leaves
     columns intact and in order in the saved JSON.

     1–2 are must-haves. Mock at the @/lib/api module boundary, per

     ---
     Questions for the backend (resolve in P2, not P7)

     1. Can a board report ever be approved today? BR13/BR15/BR21 have no
     producer, and
     bank-specific content resolves into the outline but produces nothing. If
     any of those is M,
     it sits in not_produced/awaiting_data forever and can_approve is
     permanently false —
     P7 would ship a button that always 409s. Does the gate exempt no-producer
     sections?
     2. insurer is unreachable from the UI (decision 2). An insurance company
     gets the
     corporate outline and loses whatever insurer-specific sections the backend
     can resolve.
     Either two pills is the product decision and insurer should go server-side,
     or a third
     pill is needed later — but it shouldn't stay silently unreachable.
     3. Does /profile recognise an arbitrary sector name? We send the lookup
     name verbatim. An
     unmapped name ("Diversified Holding") presumably yields generic notes and
     possibly a wrong
     derived.regulator, with nothing client-side able to detect it. Can the
     response echo
     whether the sector was recognised?
     4. PUT /outline with omitted sections — undocumented. The plan sends all 46
     to sidestep it;
     confirm a dropped section at included: false isn't a 422.
     5. GET /sections mode — /assemble documents mode: "table"|"prose";
     /sections
     doesn't. Fallback is to derive from content_type and sniff the parsed keys.
     6. cover.values keys and whether columns can nest anywhere other than the
     table root.

     Also worth a product call: banks resolve extra slots and sections whose
     producers don't exist.
     Consider shipping corporate-first with an explicit banner on bank reports,
     rather than letting
     someone upload SAMA returns into a void.

● All seven phases done. Lint clean, 253 tests pass across 19 files, build
  clean. The only typecheck errors are the four that pre-date this work.

  What shipped

  The fixtures are gone. boardReport.ts (720 lines of registry + resolver +
  fabricated report) and its 18-test file are deleted. The server owns
  resolution now.

  ┌───────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │           │                                                              │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ src/types/board.ts, src/lib/api.ts → boardReports namespace  │
  │ New       │ (17 endpoints), board-helpers.ts, BoardSetupPage.tsx,        │
  │           │ BoardReportCard.tsx                                          │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Rewritten │ BoardReportPage.tsx → /board-report/:reportId, all four      │
  │           │ steps on real endpoints                                      │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ SectionContent + EditableSectionContent (the columns fix),   │
  │ Touched   │ DownloadMenu (onDownload), ApproveConfirmDialog              │
  │           │ (title/children), App.tsx                                    │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Tests     │ 29 new: 7 columns, 20 helpers, 2 round-trip                  │
  └───────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The renderer bug is fixed — that one was live before this work. columns now
  fixes both the set and the order, so BR35's per-meeting columns and BR18's
  year columns stop being reshuffled by Object.keys, and a grid with a value
  column can't be misread as a financial statement. Existing quarterly/earnings
  payloads send no columns and behave byte-identically.

  Notable wiring decisions: the profile PATCHes on Continue, not per pill (it
  re-resolves the whole outline server-side, so per-click would discard your
  step-3 choices), and it refetches outline and sources because slot counts
  derive from issuer type. Both async runs refetch their domain data every poll
  tick — that's what makes extraction status and section status move live.
  Approve is enabled regardless of can_approve and lets the 409 answer, so the
  blockers come from the server rather than a stale flag.

  Two things I could not do

  Verify payloads against the live API. That was P2's real checkpoint and it
  needs a running backend and a token. Every type is written from the spec —
  first run against real data may need adjustment, most likely cover.values keys
  and whether GET /sections carries a mode (I derive one by sniffing the
  content).

  Answer the six backend questions. The one that matters most: BR13/BR15/BR21
  have no producer, so if any is mandatory, can_approve may never become true
  and Approve will always 409. Worth asking before anyone tries to approve a
  report.

✻ Baked for 35m 12s

※ recap: Goal was replacing the Board Report builder's fixture data with the real /api/v1/board API; all seven phases are implemented, with 253 tests and the build passing. Next: run it against the live backend to verify the payload shapes match my types.

❯ [Image #10] why you delte options                                             
  ⎿  [Image #10]

● Nothing was deleted — they moved one screen forward.

  This is the setup page (/board-report), which is the entry point you picked in
  the plan: choose a year → Create report → the builder at
  /board-report/:reportId. Issuer type, Sector and the three disclosure flags
  are step 1 of the builder, on the other side of that button.

  The reason for the split: PATCH /profile is scoped to a report id, so a report
  has to exist before it can hold a profile. Picking the year is what creates
  it.

  They're also already answered for you — POST /reports sends a profile seeded
  from your company record (reporting_sector, is_shariah, has_sukuk, sector), so
  step 1 opens pre-filled rather than blank.

  That said, POST /reports accepts the full issuer_profile in its body, so the
  profile could live on this page instead — one card with Financial year +
  Issuer type + Sector + flags, then Create. That would match the Earnings setup
  page you pointed me at, which puts type/period/tone/sources all together
  before Continue.

  Want me to pull the profile forward onto this page? Step 1 of the builder
  would then become a read-only summary you can go back and change, or disappear
  entirely and make it a three-step builder.

● Aligned to the spec. Five changes, the first being the one that actually
  mattered:

  1. Staged upload — one batch, not one call per slot. This was wrong before: I
  uploaded on file-pick, which would have 409'd on the second slot. Files are
  now held in local state as you attach them across as many slots as you like,
  and one Process documents button sends them together. The request also changed
  shape — files and slots are repeated fields matched by position, not a single
  slot field, so the earlier API doc's slot is superseded.

  2. The loader is the app's standard one. AiLoadingScreen, full-screen, same as
  the quarterly and earnings flows, with its own milestones and copy per job —
  reading documents vs writing sections. Produce drives it with real progress
  from output_summary ("21 of 37 sections · 3 skipped"); upload runs
  indeterminate because there's nothing per-file to count. My bespoke inline
  progress card is deleted.

  3. Step 4 renders from GET /sections, not /assemble. /assemble only carries
  produced sections, so it could never show what's still missing. Every included
  section now appears with its own treatment: needs_input amber with
  feeder.message saying exactly what's outstanding, empty grey with its reason,
  pending with a Produce button. /assemble is still used — for the cover and
  brand, where it's authoritative and matches the exporter.

  4. Provenance is shown. feeder.citations render as "Source: document, p.164",
  and extraction_note above it. The carried-forward banner is louder now
  (heavier amber, bordered) since it's the safeguard the approve gate depends
  on.

  5. Approve is gated on can_approve — I had it always-enabled letting the 409
  answer; the spec says gate it, so it's gated, with the 409 handling kept as
  the backstop. The readiness chips are now clickable and scroll to the first
  blocking section.

  Also: Regenerate per produced section (?regenerate=true), and it's
  deliberately not offered on empty sections or ones with no producer, where it
  could only 422.

  Lint clean on everything I touched, 253 tests pass, build clean.

✻ Baked for 8m 39s

※ recap: You're wiring the Board Report builder to the real /api/v1/board backend, and I just finished aligning it to your BOARD_REPORT_FRONTEND spec: staged batch upload, the standard AI loader, /sections-driven step 4, provenance, and a gated Approve. Next: test it against the live API.

❯  1 · Fix the duplicate-file bug (yours)                                       
                                                                                
    Your FY-2026 batch sent 03_Board_Member_Profiles.docx for both the          
    profiles slot and the compliance slot. Almost certainly one file-input ref  
    shared across slot rows. Each slot row needs its own input and its own      
    state key:                                                                  
                                                                                
    const [staged, setStaged] = useState({});   // { [slotName]: File }         
    onChange={e => setStaged(s => ({ ...s, [slot.slot]: e.target.files[0] }))}  
                                                                                
    Until that's fixed you'll now get a 422 instead of silent corruption.       
                                                                                
    2 · Handle the new 422                                                      
                                                                                
    { "detail": { "error": "The same document content was filed under           
    different slots — …",                                                       
                  "duplicates": [{ "slots": [...], "files": [...] }] } }        
                                                                                
    Show detail.error, and highlight the rows named in duplicates[].slots.      
                                                                                
    3 · Upload response field renamed                                           
                                                                                
    slot (string) → slots (array), since one call can now carry several. Only   
    matters if you read it back.                                                
                                                                                
    The request itself is backwards-compatible — slot still works for a         
    single-slot call — but for the collect-then-process flow send slots, one    
    per file, positionally matched to files.                                    
                                                                                
    4 · Clear the error banner when a run finishes                              
                                                                                
    This is why you saw "Worker restarted while pipeline was in-flight" on a    
    report whose upload had actually succeeded. Your poller kept a stale error  
    in state. On every poll result:                                             
                                                                                
    if (run.status !== "running") {                                             
      setError(run.status === "failed" ? run.error_message : null);   // clear  
    on success                                                                  
      await refetchSources();                                                   
    }                                                                           
                                                                                
    Also worth knowing                                                          
                                                                                
    report_type is now board_report, not board_pack. The /board/* endpoints     
    never take it, so this only bites if you filter the generic reports list    
    by type anywhere.

      226 + * A 422 from the upload endpoint: the same document content w

      227 + * more than one slot. Returns the message to show and the slo

      231 +export function readDuplicateSlots(err: unknown): { message: s
          +tring; slots: string[] } | null {                             
      232 +  if (!(err instanceof ApiError) || err.status !== 422) return
          + null;                                                        
      233 +  const body = (err.body ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;   
      234 +  const detail = isRec(body.detail) ? body.detail : body;     
      235 +  const dupes = Array.isArray(detail.duplicates) ? detail.dupl
          +icates : null;                                                
      236 +  if (!dupes) return null;                                    
      237 +  const slots = Array.from(                                   
      238 +    new Set(                                                  
      239 +      dupes.flatMap((d) =>                                    
      240 +        isRec(d) && Array.isArray(d.slots) ? d.slots.filter((x
          +): x is string => typeof x === 'string') : [],                
      241 +      ),                                                      
      242 +    ),                                                        
      243 +  );                                                          
      244 +  const message =                                             
      245 +    pick(detail, 'error', 'message') ??                       
      246 +    'The same document was attached to more than one slot.';  
      247 +  return { message, slots };                                  
      248 +}

● All four handled. Lint clean, 255 tests pass, build clean.

  1 · Duplicate-file bug — nothing to fix. The current build already does
  exactly what you described: each SlotRow renders its own <input type="file">
  and calls onStage(slot.slot, file), keying staged by slot name. No shared ref.
  The FY-2026 corruption came from the previous upload-on-pick build, which was
  replaced when I moved to the staged batch flow.

  2 · The 422 is handled. readDuplicateSlots reads detail.error and
  de-duplicates duplicates[].slots across groups, so a slot named in two groups
  is highlighted once. The message shows in the red banner; the named rows get a
  tinted background and a red left bar. Attaching or clearing any file resets
  the highlight. Two tests cover it, including that a 422 without a duplicates
  list isn't treated as one.

  3 · slots array added to BoardRunHandle as optional. I only read
  run_id/poll_url, so nothing else changed — the request already sends slots,
  one per file, positionally matched.

  4 · The stale error is the real bug you caught. I only ever set sourcesError
  on failure and never cleared it, so a failed attempt followed by a successful
  one still showed the old message. Both pollers now resolve the banner on every
  finished run — null on completed, the message otherwise. Same fix applied to
  the produce poller.

  board_report rename: one place was affected — the dashboard's Active Reports
  card maps report_type to a label and initials. Added board_report → "Board of
  Directors' Report" / BR, keeping board_pack so existing rows don't lose their
  label.

● You shouldn't have to. That was my bug — step 3's only primary action was
  Generate report, so stepping back to check the section list and pressing
  Continue re-ran the whole pipeline.

  Now, once anything is produced, step 3 shows:

  - View report → as the primary — just moves you to step 4, no regeneration
  - Regenerate all as a secondary, so re-running is a deliberate choice rather
  than the default

  Before anything is produced it still reads Generate report, as it should.

  I also fixed a related trap: the step-4 loader was a one-shot ref, so if that
  fetch ever failed you'd be stuck on "Nothing produced yet" forever. It's now
  guarded on "do I already have the sections" rather than "have I tried", so
  returning to the step retries — plus an explicit Retry button on the empty
  state.

  Lint clean, 255 tests pass, build clean.

● Done. Once anything is produced, both Sources and Sections go read-only:

  - Sources: no Attach, no Replace, no Remove — the document list stays visible
  with its extraction status
  - Sections: checkboxes disabled, drag handles off — the resolved list stays
  fully visible with its notes

  Each shows a green notice saying the report has been generated and pointing at
  Regenerate all on the Sections step, which stays available (until approval,
  which locks everything permanently).

  The reasoning: swapping a source document or re-ticking a section after
  generation would leave the produced content silently describing a different
  set of inputs. Locking them makes regeneration the explicit way back.

  One consequence worth deciding on: if a section came back needs_input because
  a document was missing, you now can't add that document without regenerating
  the whole report first — and regenerating won't help until the document is in.
  If that's a case you expect to hit, the fix is to keep Sources editable while
  generated and only lock Sections. Say the word and I'll split them.

  Lint clean, 255 tests pass, build clean.

● Step 2 won't let you past until every required document is in.

  - Continue is disabled while any required slot is still pending, with a
  tooltip naming them.
  - An amber notice at the top of the step names the outstanding slots outright,
  rather than making you scan the list for them.
  - The footer line reads "2 required documents still needed" in amber, or "All 
  required documents are in." once they are.
  - Process N documents is never blocked — attaching the missing files is
  exactly how you clear the gate.

  I gated on required only, not the raw 9/10 count you saw. That count includes
  optional slots, and the API sets required: true specifically when a mandatory
  section depends on the slot — so an optional document being absent shouldn't
  stop the report. If you'd rather every slot be mandatory, that's a one-word
  change.

  This also lines up with what already existed: initialStep only opens a
  reopened report on Sections once all required slots are received, so the gate
  and the landing logic agree.
